<meta>This page concludes a personal account or letter regarding a religious conversion. The author is writing from Sedan, a known Huguenot (French Protestant) stronghold at the time, explaining why he chose to write his reasons down rather than engage in public debate.</meta>

be powerful in the weak. For just as God hides His mystery from the wise and clever, but reveals it to the <margin>1 Corinthians 1</margin> simple-minded: so is the truth in the mouths of young children stronger than the lies in the mouths of all the scholars of this world. But because the present time is, unfortunately, such that I could not engage in such <term>disputation</term> <note>Formal theological debates that were common in the 17th century, often high-stakes and politically charged.</note> without placing myself in obvious danger and causing great unrest in the place where I was—and it would not have been fitting for me to enter the House of God with noise and unrest. Furthermore, today’s <term>disputations</term> <note>original: "disputationes"</note> bring forth more shouting than benefit, because people debate not to search for the truth, but to gain honor and make themselves famous.

Therefore, I have judged that it would be more appropriate and edifying if I put the reasons for my <term>conversion</term> <note>original: "bekehrung"; the author is explaining his move from one faith to another, likely Catholicism to Protestantism given the context of Sedan.</note> into writing. In this I will now labor with the help of God, since by His grace I have come to such a place where one may speak and write freely of the truth. For I recognize myself as duty-bound to give you an account and answer regarding my actions. I would consider myself blessed if the small light which the Lord has shared with me could illuminate you in your darkness; I would then gladly die, if I were to see you with me on the path of life.

Meanwhile, I pray to God, the Father of all mercy, that He grant us His grace, so that we all may be of one mind in Christ, and that He fill you with the joy and comfort of His Spirit; so that after we have honored and praised His name here on Earth, He may also make us glorious above in Heaven with the glory He prepared for us before the foundation of the world was laid.

This is the wish of the one who desires to be yours at all times and even unto the altar <note>A common historical expression of absolute loyalty, derived from the Latin "usque ad aras."</note>. Given at Sedan, the 10th of May, 1624.

->Your most humble, serviceable, and obedient son,<-
->he of Vrillac.<-

->T H E   E N D.<-

<summary>In this concluding section of his letter, the author explains that he has written down the reasons for his religious conversion to avoid the danger and vanity of public debate, finishing with a prayer for his family's enlightenment.</summary>

<meta>This page serves as the title page for the second part of a prophetic work regarding the Bohemian War (part of the Thirty Years' War). It follows the previous page’s discussion of a personal religious conversion, shifting now into a broader political and prophetic polemic.</meta>

# THE ROARING LION
->in<-
## THE SECOND PART of the Prophetic Decision regarding the Bohemian War.

# Seventeen Questions,
in which the whole Bohemian Case is decided, based on the truth and secret wisdom of God: namely, how it has transpired from the beginning until now, how it transpires now, and how it will yet transpire with Bohemia and other nations; and what each party—namely the <term>Papists</term> <note>A term used by 17th-century Protestants to refer to Roman Catholics, implying they follow the Pope rather than the Bible.</note> and those who side with the <term>Evangelicals</term> <note>At this time, "Evangelical" was a general term for Protestants (both Lutherans and Calvinists) who claimed to return to the original "Evangelium" or Gospel.</note>—can expect.

In the manner of a Bohemian Cup-bearer and a Palatine Steward <note>These titles refer to the ceremonial offices of the Holy Roman Empire. The King of Bohemia was the Arch-Cupbearer and the Elector Palatine was the Arch-Steward. This is a pointed political reference to Frederick V, the "Winter King," who held both titles.</note>. Against Doctor Hoën <note>Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg (1580–1645), a powerful Lutheran court preacher in Saxony who was a fierce polemicist against the Calvinists and the Bohemian revolt.</note> and other slanderers; what each question is, you shall find in the Index.

->*Isaiah 54, verse 17.*<-
> Every tongue that sets itself against you, you shall condemn in judgment.

## *To the Reader.*

<image-desc>An ornate woodcut initial letter 'F' decorated with floral and foliage patterns begins the first word of the text.</image-desc>Dear friendly reader / and also elsewhere blind Slanderers: It is now well-known <note>original: *notori*</note> and public to all the world / what a miserable state of affairs has taken hold in the Kingdom of Bohemia / during the ongoing course of war where not only has everything been devastated and consumed and continues to be; but also where much innocent blood has been shed / and such misery is heard that it might well move a stone in the earth to pity. But why should I say much about pity? All the world cries out / and especially our fine neighbors / the people of Meissen <note>Meissen is a region in Saxony. At this time, the Lutheran Elector of Saxony had sided with the Catholic Emperor against the Protestant Bohemians, leading to deep bitterness between these "supposed fellow believers."</note> / our supposed fellow believers / how it serves the Kingdom of Bohemia and all its inhabitants quite right, what has happened and is happening to them / and they are not even satisfied with this / but also write such defamatory and gruesome lampoons <term>Pasquils</term> <note>original: *Pasquillen*; short, anonymous satires or lampoons, often posted in public places to mock or ruin reputations.</note> / not only against the Kingdom of Bohemia / its incorporated lands <note>This refers to the "Lands of the Bohemian Crown," which included Moravia, Silesia, and Upper and Lower Lusatia.</note>, inhabitants, and friends / but also against persons of princely and high rank / such that it is worse than waste-paper. Now it is certain and indeed the case / that the Kingdom of Bohemia has so sinned against God / that it is worthy of a sharp and thorough chastisement / it is therefore only right / that we poor Bohemians bear the Lord’s wrath / for we have sinned against Him. In this case, our supposed fellow believers have no compassion for us / but rather in their devilishly-zealous anger they are so intense / that they curse and damn us / and attribute all wrongdoing to us. But so that the godless may not glory so much with the mockers / nor the mighty gain the upper hand / and so that the black Devil might not imagine himself to be entirely white / I have, through God’s gracious grant and the prompting of the Spirit of Truth, written and published a *Prophetic Decision on the Bohemian War* <note>original: *Decisionem Propheticam belli Bohemici*</note> / in the past year 1620 / even before the final invasion of Prague <note>The Battle of White Mountain occurred in November 1620, leading to the rapid occupation of Prague by Imperial forces.</note> occurred / therein, in a prophetic

<meta>manner /</meta>

<summary>In this preface, the author laments the devastation of Bohemia during the early Thirty Years' War and condemns the Lutherans of Saxony for their lack of sympathy and defamatory writings. He frames the war as a divine punishment for Bohemia's sins but announces his prophetic work as a defense against their "mockers."</summary>

...wisely / and not slanderously <note>original: *calumniose*</note> decided / this most important question / namely: with whom one should side or not side / concerning the Bohemian situation <note>The "Bohemian situation" refers to the political and religious upheaval of the Bohemian Revolt (1618–1620), which sparked the Thirty Years' War.</note> / and indeed I have directed this same question specifically against **Doctor Hoë** <note>Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg (1580–1645), a leading Lutheran theologian in Saxony who notoriously sided with the Catholic Emperor against the Protestant Bohemians.</note> / who has issued a **Babylonian question** / which the late **Polycarp** <note>Likely Polykarp Leyser the Elder (1552–1610), a prominent Lutheran theologian and predecessor to Hoë.</note> composed / which reads thus: that one should side sooner and much more with the **Papists** <note>Catholics</note> than with the **Calvinists** / understood in both spiritual and worldly matters. Now / I have indeed considered the matter in my aforementioned **First Decision** in such a way / that by God's granting I spoke of it in a prophetic manner / and read the riot act <note>original: *den Text gelesen*; literally "read the text," an idiom meaning to reprimand or lecture someone.</note> to those doctors fairly well / though still mercifully. But because there is no relenting from Doctor Hoë / but rather he believes himself to be right in his asinine persuasion / I must / following the reminder of the Spirit of Truth / continue in my **Prophetic Decision** <note>original Latin: *Decisione Prophetica*</note> and add the **second part** <note>original Latin: *Secundam partem*</note> as well.

The Lord Doctor has not yet answered me / although he did mention me in his **Ninety-Nine Lies** and "Hoë-ish" <note>A pun on his name, Hoë, and "höfisch" (courtly), or simply "Hoë-like" articles.</note> articles / and claimed that a "poisonous little treatise" had been published against him under the name of **PAUL FELGENHAUER** / calling me the "alleged Felgenhauer" / as if perhaps someone else had written it under my name. So that the Doctor may better recognize who I am / I have also wished to add this **second part** / for him and his fellow-slanderers <note>original: *mitcalumnianten*</note> / that they might see themselves rightly therein: And thereafter judge the Bohemian matter better / than those donkeys have done previously.

But I am greatly amazed / that the Lord Doctor does not answer me / perhaps he has no time. Yet he could have let another answer for him / just as **Master ANDREAS MYLIUS** did against the noble **Count Schlick** <note>Joachim Andreas von Schlick (1569–1621), a leader of the Bohemian Estates, who was executed for his role in the rebellion.</note>? Ah, if such a **busybody** <note>original Greek: *πολυπϱαγμων* (polypragmon), meaning a meddler or a person who interferes in others' affairs.</note> came to me / I would **wash that fellow's head / with Bohemian lye** <note>Lye was a harsh cleaning agent; "washing someone's head" is a classic idiom for a severe scolding.</note> / and he would owe me little thanks for it. But since the response is delayed / I must expand the "poisonous little treatise" / and take upon myself **the role of the steward and cupbearer** <note>original Latin: *vicem Dapiferi & Pincernæ*</note> / to prepare a hearty meal and precious drink for the **Babylonian, whorish womanhood** and her adulterers. From which they might all become a little merry / and drunk alongside / on Bohemian, Rhenish, and Hungarian wine. Take heed / you Babylonians / it will go foolishly before your eyes / for you must devour and guzzle / nothing but grim poison / which you...

A clever pun on the surname of his opponent, Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg, and the German words for "the high ones" (*Hohen*) and "the scornful ones" (*Höhnischen*).</note>, but rather He makes all that is high low / and lets the scornful run headlong / so that they fall / and become an object of eternal wrath and mockery.

<margin>Isaiah 2:12</margin>
The worldly-wise are fools before the LORD / and do not know the counsel and plan of the Highest / for they look only upon men and their outward appearance, which is nonetheless an abomination before GOD. Therefore, you shameful blasphemer / watch yourself / it will go very poorly for you when your slandering finally comes to an end: What will it be worth, you purveyor of lies <note>original: *Lügen-Wäscher*; literally "washer of lies," an idiom for someone who churns out or spreads falsehoods.</note> / your mouth will be stopped / and your tongue will be paralyzed / so that you must fall silent before the power of the LORD / and become an eternal mockery to all the world / then shall the wretched delight in you / and the sorrowful have joy in the LORD.

But as for this my <term>treatise</term> <note>original: *tractat*</note>, I will divide it into certain questions / wherein the matter shall be clearly and properly <term>decided</term> <note>original: *decidiret*; from the Latin *decidere*, meaning to settle a legal or theological dispute</note> and resolved / so that everyone who reads it may truly understand the affair / and pass a different <term>judgment</term> <note>original: *judicium*</note> on the Bohemian matters / for as the Scripture says: Render a true judgment / do not oppress the poor in the gate / and do not pass unjust and perverted verdicts. Let no one cling to men / but rather to GOD'S wisdom / so shall he also perceive rightly / John 7:24, Proverbs 22:22-23, Psalm 82:3, Leviticus 19.

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues Paul Felgenhauer's theological analysis of the Bohemian Revolt. Following his opening attack on Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg, he now shifts to the "First Question" of his treatise: why God allowed Bohemia to be defeated and devastated.</meta>

<page-num>5.</page-num>
<header>I.</header>

# Why GOD Has Thus Punished the Bohemians: What the Cause May Be:

There is no one so coarse and lacking in understanding that he would say Bohemia was thus destroyed by God without guilt, for that would be quite foolish. Therefore, one must consider the transaction correctly and judge according to the Scriptures—especially in this final time, in such miraculous cases—since the Prophets and also the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation</note> prophesy quite a lot concerning the end times, from which the divinely wise <note>Scholars of theology and scripture</note> can recognize much and indeed the most important things that shall happen and are happening in these last days. But everything comes from the LORD; and as the Prophet says, there is no evil in the city that the LORD does not bring about, <term>Amos 3, verse 6.</term> Therefore, God Himself is the <term>principal cause</term> <note>original: *causa principalis*</note>. Here many will say: "That is Calvinist <note>A reference to the theological debate over predestination and God's role in earthly suffering, which Felgenhauer's opponents often labeled as "Calvinist" to discredit him</note> and as abominable as anything else." But no, let this serve you: This principal cause has, on the other hand, another cause which moves and occasions God to direct the evil in the city: namely, our abominable and great sins.

It had, to be sure, the outward appearance as if Jerusalem were destroyed for the reason that it did not want to serve, obey, and be subject to Nebuchadnezzar. For it wanted to be free from foreign dominion, and that was also quite right, according to political wisdom and freedom. But the Scripture assigns a different cause to the person of Jerusalem and says: "Our iniquity and our fathers' iniquity" (<term>Lamentations 3</term> <note>original: *Thren. 3*, referring to the Latin *Threni* or Lamentations of Jeremiah</note>) weighs upon us; there is the cause of destruction. It is exactly the same with us poor Bohemians as well. For indeed, our great sins have caused God to let us be destroyed by fierce enemies. He hid His face from us, and our prayer could not reach His ears; for our sins separate God and us from one another.

There is no need for a <term>specific enumeration</term> <note>original: *Specificirens*</note> of what kind of sins they be, for they are more numerous than the sand by the sea, as abominable and terrifying as any sins of the godless, for which we must rightly feel shame if we were to recount them. For they will not be lesser than those of Sodom and Gomorrah. The masters and the servants have sinned, the young and the old, and not a single one is found who did good and sought God—not even one; we are all—

<sig>A iij</sig>
<meta>catchword: especially.</meta>

<summary>Felgenhauer argues that the fall of Bohemia was not a political accident but a divine punishment for the nation's overwhelming sins, drawing parallels between the Bohemians and the biblical fall of Jerusalem.</summary>
<keywords>Bohemian Revolt, divine punishment, sin, Jerusalem, Nebuchadnezzar, Amos, Lamentations, Thirty Years' War, theology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the theological justification for Bohemia's suffering. Felgenhauer uses the catchword "they" (sie) to transition into the next page, which likely describes the enemies' intentions toward the Bohemian people.</meta>

<page-num>6.</page-num>

...partly incompetent and evildoers. In summary: all these sins that the Prophets recount regarding Jerusalem are also found among us, and no less so. Therefore, let us confess our iniquity and that of our fathers, so that we do not conceal them. The LORD has prepared an evil for us in all our cities; He has shot His arrows into us, which still stick in us with fire-burning wrath. He is enraged like a lion; He tears and tramples; He flies over us like an eagle and a bird of prey, and watches with His sharp eyes how He might catch us, make a carcass out of us, and an abomination <note>original: *Unflath*; referring to something foul or filthy</note> which stinks before all nations in all lands. Therefore we must now do penance in ashes along with Jerusalem and let the hard driver <note>A reference to the oppressive military or political forces occupying Bohemia</note> rule over us until the time is up, when the LORD shall again have mercy on us and raise us up from the dust, and repay our enemies. Therefore we say with Jerusalem: <margin>Micah 7, v. 7 <note>Felgenhauer likely means Micah 7:8-9 based on the text provided</note></margin>

> I will bear the wrath of the LORD, for I have sinned against Him, until He pleads my cause and executes judgment for me. He will bring me forth to the light, so that I may see my delight in His grace.

Now understand the matter correctly, what I say: God has justly punished us Bohemians because of our sins, but He does not wish to destroy us utterly; rather, He will certainly raise us up again, and though He slays us, He will yet awaken us on the third day. <margin>Hosea 6, v. 2</margin> Look to it, you other nations: see what kind of end it will have with us, and what kind of beginning it will have for you. When the LORD has fulfilled His wrath upon us, it shall likewise happen to you as it has to us, and even much more—especially to you who now mock us. The ignorant take up the Bohemian affair <term>Handel</term> <note>In this context, *Handel* refers to the political and military conflict of the Bohemian Revolt</note> thus and say: "The Bohemians have sinned grossly against the Emperor, therefore they are rightly punished." But those wise in God judge thus: The Bohemians have sinned, and the Emperor has also. Now God punishes both, but in an unequal manner: one part He will raise up again quite gloriously; the other part He will utterly root out and destroy. But He does it thus: namely, He punishes one with and through the other, and lets both sides run their course, so that no one knows except the wise what end it will finally reach. For the LORD does nothing unless He reveals His secret to His servants the prophets. <margin>Amos 3, v. 7</margin> But the wise say thus of this affair and its outcome: namely, Bohemia will be held captive by its enemies before all the world, and they will be minded...

<summary>Felgenhauer compares the suffering of Bohemia to that of biblical Jerusalem, arguing that while the Bohemians are being punished for their sins, their enemies (including the Emperor) will face an even more total destruction. He views the current political catastrophe as a divine "correction" that will eventually lead to a glorious restoration.</summary>

<keywords>Bohemian Revolt, Thirty Years' War, divine punishment, Emperor, Jerusalem, prophecy, Micah, Hosea, Amos, penance</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's argument regarding the divine punishment of Bohemia. On the previous page, the author compared Bohemia's plight to that of biblical Jerusalem, urging the people to bear God's wrath until the appointed time of mercy. He now transitions into a more direct political and legal defense of the Bohemian cause.</meta>

<page-num>7.</page-num>

...to devour them all. But just as the beast intends to tear and trample the saints, their blood will splash into its eyes; because of this, it will become blind, so that it no longer knows what to do. Also, its feet will become slippery and slide on the blood of the saints; then God will again have mercy on us and destroy our driver along with all his helpers. Pay attention to this, dear reader: if this truth turns out to be a lie, then the devil brought it forth in me, and not the Spirit of God. Thus we speak **theologically** <term>theologically; from the perspective of divine will</term> of these matters, and from knowledge of the Scripture **in regard to God** <term>original: *respectu Dei*</term>, not in an external, foolish manner, nor even **politically** <term>politically; concerning secular law and governance</term>.

Rather, we **Bohemians** <term>Bohemians; people of the Kingdom of Bohemia</term> recognize ourselves as unjust before God—before God, I say! But not before the **Emperor** <term>original: *Käyser*; referring to the Holy Roman Emperor</term>; mark this very well, for now we wish to speak of it **politically** <term>politically</term> in a different way than the blind **doctors** <term>original: *Doctores*; likely referring to court theologians and legal scholars</term> do. Accordingly, the Bohemian situation is not to be considered merely within its own time, as perhaps other wars in former times; no, it is something much different, which occurs at the end of time, about which neither **Doctor Hoe** <term>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), a prominent Lutheran court preacher in Saxony who opposed the Bohemian Revolt</term> nor other ignorant people can **judge** <term>original: *judiciren*</term> anything. Whoever understands the prophets well will also **judge** <term>judge</term> this rightly.

For the **Lord** will judge among all nations, and precisely those who think they judge others rightly will bear the most guilt. Furthermore, if we indeed wish to speak **politically** <term>politically</term> about it, and consider the Bohemian cause according to external appearance, the question arises: whether, even according to common law, in a fair manner, with good reason and justice, **Jerusalem** <note>The author uses Jerusalem as an allegory for Bohemia</note> was invaded by **Nebuchadnezzar** <note>An allegory for the invading Imperial forces</note>. To this the answer is **negative** <term>original: *negativè*</term>, namely, that **Nebuchadnezzar** did it out of pure hatred, envy, and **pride** <term>original: *Hofarth*</term>, as he was a haughty king who oppressed **Jerusalem** and wanted to make it **hereditarily** <term>original: *Erblich*; referring to hereditary rule vs. elective monarchy</term> subject to him, though it was a free kingdom. For just as he did not want to be subject to another, so also should he not have desired, forced, or made others **hereditarily** <term>hereditarily</term> subject to him; for it is also said **politically** <term>politically</term>, as nature teaches us: **What you do not want done to yourself, do not do to another.** <note>original: "*Quod tibi non vis fieri, alteri ne feceris.*" A version of the Golden Rule often used in legal contexts.</note>

Accordingly, **politically** <term>politically</term>, **Nebuchadnezzar** did entirely wrong in oppressing **Jerusalem**, since he had no significant cause against them. **Jerusalem** was for itself a completely free kingdom, which might choose for itself whatever king it wanted, and was obligated to no one to become **hereditary** <term>hereditary</term> property—not even to the royal **lineage** <term>original: *Stamb*; family line</term> of their own people, let alone to one unequal in their **religion** <term>religion</term> and a stranger. There was not only no **right of succession** <term>original: *jus successionis*</term>, much less a **hereditary** <term>hereditary</term> claim, but simply a **free election** <term>original: *libera electio*</term>,

<summary>The author distinguishes between Bohemia's "theological" guilt before God and its "political" innocence before the Emperor, using the biblical allegory of Jerusalem and Nebuchadnezzar to argue that Bohemia is a free, elective monarchy that has been unjustly invaded.</summary>
<keywords>Bohemian Revolt, elective monarchy, sovereignty, Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg, Nebuchadnezzar, Jerusalem, political theology, Golden Rule, right of succession</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author’s political and theological defense of the Bohemian Estates. Following the previous page’s distinction between being "unjust before God" but "just before the Emperor," the author now uses the biblical example of King Rehoboam to justify the right of a people to reject a ruler who violates their established laws and follows poor counsel.</meta>

<page-num>8.</page-num>

...a free choice / where they had the power / to elect a king for themselves / whomever they themselves wanted / especially if he would not accommodate himself to them / as indeed happened with the Ten Tribes / 2 Chronicles 10. They fell away from the House of David / for this reason / that the young, arrogant Rehoboam <note>Rehoboam was the son of Solomon; his refusal to lighten the labor tax led to the rebellion of the ten northern tribes of Israel.</note> / who took counsel with the young boys he had grown up with / wanted to make the yoke heavier for the people / than his father had done / and immediately strike them with scorpion-whips. Thus God punished the young King Rehoboam, so that all of Israel fell away from him / except for the tribes of Judah and Benjamin. This freedom of choice belonged to the people of Israel / so that they did not wish to confirm the already designated king over them / even though he was the rightful heir in that regard / and this was because he received a **Jesuit-maddened devil’s rule** <note>original: *Jesu-wütische Teuffels Regel*; a derogatory term combining "Jesuit" and "rabid/mad," reflecting the era's intense religious friction.</note> from the counsel of his young brothers / who had gone to school and grown up with him / but he removed the old councilors from their offices / and despised their faithful advice. 

Now, take this event before you / and examine the **Bohemian Affair** <term>original: *Böhmischer Handel*; the 1618 revolt of the Bohemian Estates against the Habsburgs</term> from a political perspective / and you will see clearly / who is right or wrong / to which end the Bohemians' extensive **Apology** <note>The *Apologia* was a formal legal and political defense published by the Bohemian Estates to justify their rejection of Ferdinand II.</note> may serve, which protests in a lawful manner.

But how was it with Nebuchadnezzar, and the King of Assyria? Did they do right / when they overran Jerusalem? No / our Lord God raised them up over His people / for the sake of the sins / that Jerusalem had committed / so Jerusalem was guilty before the Lord / not before those kings. Thus understand / what **Politics** <term>original: *Politia*; referring to the science of governance and the legal state</term> is / how it has its own laws / what kind of respect, relation, and condition exists / in its statutes and privileges / where one must be referred and held against the other. In this regard, Rehoboam with his **Jesuit-maddened fools** <note>original: *Jesu-wütischen Roßlöffeln*; "Roßlöffel" is a derogatory term for a coarse or foolish person.</note> was very unwise and unjust / when he rejected the old councilors / and understood **Politics** <term>Politia</term> just as little / as do the honorless, frivolous Jesuit-madmen of today / who are true murderers of policy / and thieves of government / they have better deserved the gallows and the sword / than many a poor soldier / whom **Wallenstein** <note>Albrecht von Wallenstein (1583–1634), the famous and ruthless military commander of the Imperial forces during the Thirty Years' War.</note> had hanged and executed.

Similarly, how did it go with Ahab and Jezebel / who killed the prophets of the Lord? Was it not so / that Ahab let himself be regulated and ruled by a woman? Note well why I say this. So it also went in the beginning / before the noise started in Bohemia / and yet **Doctor Matz of Dresden** <note>A mocking nickname for Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), the Chief Court Preacher in Dresden, who supported the Emperor against the Bohemian Protestants.</note> is still not so wise...

### II.

### Whether the Bohemians also committed a **Crime against Majesty** <term>original: *Crimen læsæ Majestatis*; high treason or an offense against the dignity of the sovereign</term> / and whether they had the power to make use of their freedom?

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'D' featuring floral and foliate scrollwork within a square frame.</image-desc>
This is a very important question / and indeed somewhat touched upon in the preceding / but it must be made more detailed here. However, if we wish to decide this question correctly / then we must distinguish / the right of the **Authority** <term>original: *Obrigkeit*; the divinely ordained governing body or magistracy</term> and its power / as well as the right of the subjects and their freedom / how far each extends.

We wish to speak plainly from the Scriptures and hear what we owe to the Authority. Thus the great Apostle exhorts in **Romans 13** / everyone be subject to the Authority / that has power over him / for there is no authority except from GOD / but where authority is / it is ordained by God / Whoever now sets himself against the Authority / resists GOD’S ordinance / but those who resist / shall receive a judgment upon themselves / for the powerful are not to be feared for good works but for evil: If you do not wish to fear the Authority / then do good / so you shall have praise from the same / for she is GOD’S servant. For your good / but if you do evil / then be afraid / for she does not bear the sword in vain /

But whoever helps to suppress the poor, God will in turn suppress on the Day of Judgment, and there, neither Emperor nor learned Doctor <note>Referring to academic authorities in law or theology who justified the suppression.</note> shall have any standing.

## II.

### Whether the Bohemians have committed High Treason <note>original: *Crimen læsæ Majestatis*</note>, and whether they had the power to make use of their freedom?

This is a very important question, and while it was touched upon somewhat in the preceding section, it must be examined more thoroughly here. However, if we wish to decide this question correctly, we must distinguish between the rights of the governing authority <note>original: *Obrigkeit*</note> and its power, and likewise the rights of the subjects and their freedom, and how far each of these extends.

We wish to speak plainly from the Scriptures and hear what we owe to the governing authority. Thus, the great Apostle exhorts in Romans 13: *Let everyone be subject to the governing authorities <note>original: *Obrigkeit*</note> who have power over him, for there is no authority except from **GOD**; and where authority exists, it is ordained by God. Whoever, therefore, sets himself against the authority, resists the ordinance of **GOD**; and those who resist shall receive a judgment upon themselves. For those in power are not to be feared for good works, but for evil. Do you wish not to fear the authority? Then do what is good, and you shall have praise from the same, for she is the servant of **GOD** for your benefit. But if you do evil, then be afraid, for she does not bear the sword in vain...*

<meta>catchword: She is</meta>

<summary>The author argues that the Bohemian Revolt was caused by the Emperor's refusal to hear Protestant grievances, effectively handing them over to their enemies. He begins a legal defense against the charge of high treason by citing Romans 13 to define the divinely ordained limits and duties of government.</summary>
<keywords>Bohemian Revolt, High Treason, Romans 13, Protestantism, religious persecution, Bishop of Prague, Apologia, legal defense, Thirty Years' War</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the legal and theological defense of the Bohemian Estates. Following the previous page’s discussion of the oppression of Protestants and the failure of the Emperor to provide a fair hearing, this section transitions into a formal "decision" on the rights of authorities versus the rights of subjects, grounded in the New Testament (Romans 13 and 1 Peter 2).</meta>

She is GOD’S servant / an avenger for punishment over him who does evil. Therefore, be subject now out of necessity / not only for the sake of punishment, but also for the sake of conscience; for this reason you must also give tax <note>original: *Schoß*; an archaic term for a tax or tribute paid to a sovereign</note> / for they are GOD’S servants who are to maintain such protection. And Peter says how one should be subject to every human ordinance for the LORD’S sake / to the kind master as well as the difficult <note>original: *wunderlichen*; in the context of 1 Peter 2:18, this refers to harsh, perverse, or capricious rulers</note> one / 1 Peter 2. In short: Fear GOD / and honor the King. Now we wish to see how the decision <note>original: *Decision*</note> is to be made.

Firstly, the right of the governing authority <term>Obrigkeit: the established political power, such as a prince or magistrate</term> is this: namely, that she is ordained by God as a servant who rules over the subjects <note>original: *Vnterthanen*</note>, whom she protects, punishes the wicked, represents the pious, and as a servant of God dispenses justice to one as well as the other / she does not regard the outward appearance of the person / but shows herself to be a servant of GOD. In this, it is to be understood how far the rule of the authority extends / namely, only over the external, political <note>original: *Politischen*</note> person according to the realm <note>original: *region*</note>, but not over the internal person and conscience according to religion <note>original: *Religion*</note>.

Secondly, the right of the subjects against the authority is that they have the power to seek protection from the same / for that is precisely why they pay tax <note>original: *Schoß*</note>. And if protection is not granted to them, then the authority becomes a servant of the devil, who lets injustice reign. In this case, a Christian should not withdraw from subjection, but be subject for the honor of the LORD, even to a difficult authority. But here a great crux <note>original: *Knack*</note> is to be noted / namely, what right the subjects have in matters of civil governance <note>original: *Policey*; referring here to the secular administration and legal order</note>, not theologically <note>original: *Theologicè*</note>, where as Christians they should gladly even die for Christ's sake. This, however, is the right: namely, that there is a difference between a common subject and an entire kingdom that chooses for itself a head and authority of its own free will. Where such an authority does not uphold the civil governance <note>original: *Policey*</note>, then the subject is no longer bound to render further obedience, especially in matters that concern conscience and religion <note>original: *Religion*</note>. Likewise, there is a difference between free subjects and kingdoms, and between hereditary subjects, serfs <note>original: *Leibeigenen*</note>, and proprietary lands. All sensible people who wish to speak of these dealings must take this distinction very carefully into account / for otherwise no correct decision on our question can follow.

Let us now return to the governing authority <term>Obrigkeit: the established political power or magistracy</term> and examine its rule. It acts in a truly devilish manner when it forces its subjects—even those who are serfs—into a foreign religion. In such a case, the authority not only fails to protect them but actually strikes them all dead together, condemns them, and drives them away. Thus, the authority offends and commits a **crime of high treason against divine majesty** <note>original Latin: *Crimen læsæ Majestatis divinæ*</note>. However, when an elected authority, established by free estates <note>original: *freyen Ständen*; the representative ranks of society, such as nobility and clergy, who held political rights</note>, does not protect its subjects, it strips itself of its own right. It breaks the **relationship** <note>original: *relation*</note> that exists between itself and the subjects and causes the subjects to fall away from it, just as was told earlier regarding **Rehoboam** <note>A biblical King of Israel whose refusal to lighten the burdens of his people led to a rebellion and the fracturing of his kingdom</note>. Such a ruler keeps no faith, no promise, nor any **formal pledge** <note>original: *revers*; a written legal obligation</note>; he becomes a servant of injustice and destroys all his own **reputation** and justice. For the Scripture also requires this of the governing authority: that it dispenses justice to everyone, not that it becomes a servant of the devil, destroying everything, killing, and chasing people away, just like the dragon and the devil.

On this foundation, however, stands the civil order <term>Policey: the organized governance and social well-being of a state</term> of free and non-hereditary kingdoms. Namely: so long as you, King, will not **confirm our privileges**, protect us in what is right and fair, and allow us to remain in our religion, we cannot elect you as our authority. And where you do not keep faith, we are also no longer subject to you. For this is the "bloom" and good condition of a civil order: when faith is kept, everything is beautiful and peaceful. But where this is broken, the other party is no longer indebted to the one who did not keep the faith—especially in free kingdoms. I could certainly conclude here from the Scripture and say: "Oh, it says one should be subject to the gentle master as well as the difficult <note>original: *wunderlichen*; capricious or harsh</note> one; there should be no resistance." Yes, that is right; however, this command speaks of **private persons**, not of free estates, entire kingdoms, and lands. Otherwise, the Protestants <note>original: *Evangelischen*</note> could never have freed themselves from the yoke of the Popish Emperor and the Pope. Likewise, this passage does not mean that one should let the authority rule over conscience and religion, for one must obey God more than men, according to **Acts 5:29**. If this passage were to be understood **simply** <note>original Latin: *simpliciter*; meaning absolutely or without qualification</note>, then Christians would be allowed no political privileges, much less be allowed to use their rights. Therefore, if you wish to be King in free lands and over free estates, then conduct yourself accordingly—namely, as their privileges dictate. If you will not keep them, then you cannot be King. Indeed, some "donkeys" say: "Who would want to be King of the Bohemians?" since...

...as they wish, if only someone would talk like that. Oh no, fellow! Bohemia had kings long before you and I were born, and neither they nor I need to worry about this, for none among us will become king.

When an uprising occurs in a kingdom, one must observe which party is the first to break the **relationship** <note>original: *relation*; the reciprocal legal bond between ruler and subject</note>: the governing authority or the subjects. The guilty party is the one who has forfeited and lost the rights that the other party owed them. Regarding how it went in the beginning—partly as **Jezebel** <note>A biblical reference to the wicked Queen Jezebel who was thrown from a window; here used as a provocative metaphor for the 1618 Defenestration of Prague</note> went flying out the window—with the Protestants and the Emperor, this is testified by the Bohemian *Apologia* and by experience: namely, that the Protestants not only received no audience, but had no protection at all. Indeed, they faced only hostile Imperial orders and actions from the Catholics.

Now the question arises: who has administered his office correctly? Who is the one resisting, who has committed a **crime of high treason** <note>original Latin: *Crimen læsæ Majestatis*</note>, and who has acted as God’s servant? Did the Bohemians do it? Did they violate the **civil order** <note>original: *Policey*; referring here to the general organized state of the commonwealth</note> and commit high treason? "Yes, yes," say all the **slanderers** <note>original: *Calumnianten*</note>, "the high authority was offended, and their envoys were cast out of the window!" They claim this was entirely wrong and against the Scriptures, which say... one should also obey the envoys (1 Peter 2:14). Yes, it is true; but what if those envoys want to rule over the conscience, over religion, and execute the opposing side? What if they serve the devil and do not protect, but rather offend, taking their neighbor’s freedom by force, tearing down their houses of God, driving away their priests, and pushing them out of their offices? Is that also right? Is it good? Or should the clergy simply suffer it and have no power to exercise their privileges? Indeed, in political terms it is right and well said: **it is permitted to repel force with force** <note>original Latin: *Vim vi repellere licet*</note>; otherwise, the Turk and the Pope would have devoured us long ago.

Furthermore, what of the fact that the **Letter of Majesty** <note>original: *Majestethbrief*; the 1609 charter granted by Emperor Rudolf II guaranteeing religious freedom to the Bohemian estates</note> is not upheld? High-ranking leaders are pushed into the pit for political reasons, faith is not kept, and promises are broken. In doing so, the ruler strips himself of his own majesty and reputation and commits a crime, even though he is meant to be a servant of God—and like God, who keeps faith forever (Psalm 146:6). Oh, I say, what a fine "civil order" it is when one follows the devilish hypocrites, the **raging Jesuits** <note>original: *Jesu-wüten*; a derogatory pun combining "Jesuit" with the German word for "rage" or "fury"</note>, who believe they are not bound to keep faith, especially with heretics. Who then has committed a **crime of high treason**?

Is it Bohemia or the Emperor who has acted against the former authority and against GOD? One can easily slander, disgrace, and revile, but liars do not speak the truth. Indeed, not only was no external protection provided, but not even a miserable audience was granted; and to top it all off, there was violent persecution from the opposing side and an assault on their divine worship, etc. Which is a twofold crime of high treason <note>original Latin: *Crimen læsæ Majestatis*</note> and for all eternity can never be declared right before God and men. The children of the Babylonian Whore <note>A common Protestant pejorative for the Roman Catholic Church, referencing the Whore of Babylon from the Book of Revelation</note> have torn down our churches, contrary to the Letter of Majesty <note>The *Majestätsbrief* of 1609, a document signed by Emperor Rudolf II granting religious tolerance to Bohemian Protestants</note>, and claimed they were built on the Archbishop's lands, and therefore they had the right and power to do so. Now I would like to know, from a theological perspective <note>original: *Theologicè*</note>, whether a bishop can own landed estates, and hereditary ones at that, given they have neither wives nor children—indeed, they are themselves fornicators and adulterers. But to speak of it politically <note>original: *Politicè*</note>, the lands belong to the Royal Chamber <note>the state treasury</note> and not to the Papal whoremaster.

Furthermore, if one makes use of one's privileges politically, must that be a crime of high treason? If one repels force with force, must that also be a crime of high treason? Yes, I can well imagine what a "fine" civil order <note>original: *Policey*; referring to the organized state of the commonwealth</note> that would be in a free kingdom, if one allowed oneself to be regulated according to the whim of every Papal adulterer, without even saying, "Pope, what are you doing?" <note>original Latin: *Papa quid facis*</note>. Oh no! Even if the lion <note>The Double-Tailed Lion, the heraldic symbol of Bohemia</note> lies low for now, he can certainly rise again. Just let the mice dance bravely upon him; I'll wager he will fall upon them once more.

How then should one be subject to the sovereign lords and their envoys, and how far does their right extend? Namely this: when the governors demand taxes <note>original: *Schoß*</note>, interest, and rents, and other proper debts <note>original Latin: *debita*</note>, then one should be obedient for the sake of conscience, so that no one resists. But if they wish to lead our religion astray and take it from us, and the privileges along with it, then it is as the Apostles said: one must obey God more than men (Acts 5:29). And even though the authorities in Jerusalem forbade any preaching whatsoever about the crucified Jesus, they had no right to do so; for these were matters of faith affecting the conscience, in which neither Emperor nor King should interfere, if he wishes to avoid committing high treason <note>here meaning treason against the Divine Majesty of God</note>. But if the Emperor believes he can dictate that one should not use one's freedoms nor have power, but must simply <note>original Latin: *simpliciter*</note> be subject in all things, with nothing excepted, then [fit]—

...ly recognized / all Imperial and Kingdom privileges, all [the] Princes and Estates / and the Emperor would not only have to have outgrown them as the head / but would also have to grant no freedom, privileges, rights, nor statutes to others. Oh, you fine politicians <note>original: "Pol[icey]"—referring to those who manage civil governance</note>, how well you understand governance <term>Policey</term> <note>The early modern concept of "Policey" referred to the total regulation of a community to ensure order, morality, and the common good, rather than just modern law enforcement.</note>. If one had provided fair governance / as a servant of God / if one had respected the privileges / not broken the ancestors' Letter of Majesty / not pushed the innocent out of their offices against the privileges / not attacked religion with force / given contrary orders against the innocent / and handed the government over to the Jesuits <note>original: "Jesuwiatern," a pejorative spelling</note>, to whom it does not belong at all / then the free Estates would not have had to resort to defense <note>original: "Defension"</note>. Who then started the harm <term>lædere</term> <note>original Latin: "to harm" or "to offend"</note> first? Are you fellows <note>original: "Kerllaten," likely a derogatory term for charlatans or common fellows</note> now such brave, noble people and so highly learned? Well then, refute me and put me to shame, so that one may see [your] innocence and praise your great wisdom.

Finally, if one should not have the power in governance to use one's freedom, but instead be as submissive as a serf-slave whom one buys from the Turks / then I would like to know whether such a "policy" [relationship] could even exist between a lord and subjects / if it had neither privileges nor statutes? It would be the deepest servitude and bondage / and it would have to be a "beautiful" kingdom / the likes of which I have never seen. Although Bohemia is now conquered, it did not happen through the beast’s own [power] / but by God's providence; but it will not last long /

<meta>This page continues the legal and theological defense of the Bohemian Estates. Following the previous discussion on the Emperor's failure to uphold the Letter of Majesty, the author now defines the nature of treason and addresses accusations regarding the Bohemians' true motives.</meta>

<page-num>18.</page-num>

God will turn back to us and utterly destroy our enemies. For when the **Beast** <note>A reference to the Book of Revelation; in Protestant polemics of this era, the "Beast" was often identified with the Papacy or the oppressive Catholic forces.</note> has achieved victory over the Saints, it shall also be utterly destroyed.

However, one thing remains to be addressed: namely, that one should properly know what constitutes a true <term>High Treason</term> <note>original: "Crimen læsæ Majestatis" — a crime against the majesty of the sovereign.</note> against the authorities, which is worthy of the forfeiture of life and all property, and which is to be punished with the ultimate <term>penalty</term> <note>original: "pœn"</note>. Thus, it is <term>High Treason</term> when subjects, outside of their **political** <note>original: "Politicè"</note> privileges, violently resist the authorities against their conscience; or when they blaspheme and shame a pious authority innocently and without scriptural grounds, out of their own foolhardiness and defiant, godless presumption. Similarly, it is also a <term>Crime against Divine Majesty</term> committed by the authorities when they do not protect their subjects against God’s commandment, but rather cast them out innocently, and finally even seize hold of their conscience, venturing to resist God. From these two points, any sensible person may recognize the true nature of <term>High Treason</term>, and who is guilty of it or not.

---

### III.

## Whether the Bohemians were concerned with territory—or rather with religion?

There has been such idle chatter among the <term>slanderers</term> <note>original: "Calumnianten"</note> against the Bohemians that it is a shame; they have tried to basely suggest that the Bohemians did not rightfully plead the cause of religion in their <term>Apology</term> <note>The "Apologia" was the official justification published by the Bohemian Estates after the Defenestration of Prague in 1618.</note>, but rather that they wished to be "Free Lords," to possess the **region**, and thus be subject to no authority. But this **discourse** fits together **like a fist on an eye** <note>A German idiom meaning something does not fit at all, or is completely nonsensical.</note>, where the asses always want to understand and judge all things, yet often do not know how to decide the smallest matter.

Now, as to whether the Bohemians desired the **region**, the truth must speak for itself. For if they were guilty of this, they would have undertaken such a thing long ago. But the reality is quite different: namely, it is the opposing party, the Papists, who have desired the **region**; for that reason, they have also been entrusted with the supreme command, whereas on the other hand the

<vocab>Bohemians, Religion, High Treason, Slanderers, Apology, Region/Territory, Authority</vocab>

<summary>The author defines high treason as a reciprocal violation, occurring either when subjects resist lawful authority or when rulers violate God’s laws by attacking their subjects' consciences. He further defends the Bohemian Estates against accusations that their revolt was a territorial power grab rather than a defense of religious freedom.</summary>
<keywords>High Treason, Bohemian Revolt, religious liberty, political privileges, Papists, Apologia, Thirty Years' War, Lese-majesty</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument regarding the motivations of the Bohemian Estates, specifically refuting the claim that their "Defense" was politically rather than religiously motivated. It addresses the systematic displacement of Protestants and the polemical attacks by Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg.</meta>

<page-num>16.</page-num>

**Evangelicals** <note>In this context, "Evangelicals" refers to the Protestant inhabitants of Bohemia, including both Lutherans and members of the Unity of the Brethren.</note> were ousted from their offices; against the privileges of the same, these positions were filled with **Papist** persons, and otherwise all sorts of petty "sins" were manufactured so that the Evangelicals might be boldly vexed. The **Jesuit** and **Papal** clergy had to be permitted to purchase many large estates, so that they could bring more and more of the regions under their control. In all of this, the poor Evangelicals were content and let it pass, until finally their religion was also attacked, such that their churches were torn down and their priests chased away. This is how they acted, and for that reason, the **Bohemians** committed themselves to their **Defense** <term>Defension</term> <note>original: "Defension" — the formal organized resistance and legal protection of religious rights established by the Bohemian Estates.</note>.

Indeed, it is well known and public enough how the Bohemians have been dealt with regarding religion for many years now; how they have been oppressed; what effort it cost before the **Letter of Majesty** <term>Majestätsbrief</term> <note>The 1609 charter signed by Emperor Rudolf II granting religious freedom to the Bohemian Protestants.</note> was produced; and how they were trifled with. Did they not strive day and night to find a way to undermine the Evangelicals, and especially the persons of high standing first? Now, it must all be a lie, and our neighbors and fellow believers, the **Meißner** <note>People from Meissen or Saxony; specifically referring to the Lutheran court in Dresden which was reluctant to support the Bohemian cause.</note>, say that it was nonetheless not **for the sake of religion** <note>original: "propter Religionem"</note>, just as **Doctor Hoë** <note>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), a prominent and controversial Lutheran court preacher in Saxony who strongly opposed the Calvinist influence in the Bohemian Revolt.</note> also argues and calls the Bohemians "enemies of courage." 

I ask Doctor Hoë in his conscience, however, whether he never sensed (back when he was still in Prague) that the Papists always hated the Evangelicals for the sake of religion? For otherwise, had they also been Papists, would they have been so pursued? Is it then all a lie, what the Bohemians put forward **concerning religion** <note>original: "de religione"</note>? Must the Lutherans now also help with this, so that the Papists are proven right? O, you young **Babylonian Adulterer** <note>A harsh polemical insult, likely comparing Hoë to the biblical "Whore of Babylon" for his perceived betrayal of Protestant unity.</note>, how you hide your whore’s brow, how shamefully you slander your neighbor! But it shall be repaid to you fairly.

The Babylonian Adulterer betrays himself, however, in that he has renewed a "Babylonian question," in which it is supposedly proven that one should hold with the Papists sooner and more often than with the so-called **Calvinists**. Regarding religion, the Doctor has written so much against the Calvinists out of envy, and has quickly hatched **ninety-nine lied-about articles** <note>original: "Hosen Artickell" — likely referring to the "Hosemannian Articles," a set of polemical theological points used by Lutherans to attack Calvinist doctrine.</note> and more, with which he gives nothing else to understand than that he is vexed that the Bohemians have elected a supposed "Calvinist King" <note>Referring to Frederick V of the Palatinate, known as the "Winter King," whose Calvinist faith alienated many strict Lutherans.</note>.

<summary>This page argues that the Bohemian Revolt was a necessary defense against the systematic suppression of Protestantism and the violation of the Letter of Majesty. It specifically attacks the Lutheran theologian Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg for his polemical claims that the conflict was not religious and for his preference for Catholics over Calvinists.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the argument from page 16, addressing the accusations that the Bohemian unrest was political rather than religious. It specifically critiques the pro-Imperial stance of certain Lutheran theologians in Saxony who prioritized opposing Calvinism over supporting their fellow Protestants in Bohemia.</meta>

<page-num>17.</page-num>

...thus they betray themselves regarding their true intentions for religion, showing they would much rather choose and keep a **Papist** <note>A term used by Protestants to refer to Roman Catholics, implying primary loyalty to the Pope.</note> as their leader than a **Calvinist**. If it is not about religion, then why is there so much strife because of it? In the year [16]17, people celebrated the jubilee against the Pope <note>Refers to the 100th anniversary of the Reformation in 1617.</note>, yet afterward they made alliances with his adulterers <note>Likely a polemical reference to political alliances between Lutheran powers and Catholic Imperial forces, viewed by the author as spiritual betrayal.</note>, and finally allowed it to be publicly declared that one should stand with them rather than with others. Is this what it means to be "Purely Evangelical and Lutheran"? I believe that if **Luther** himself were to examine his people in **Meissen** and **Saxony**, he would make **Doctor Hoe** <note>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), the influential court preacher in Saxony known for his intense hostility toward Calvinism, which often led him to favor the Catholic Emperor over Reformed Protestant factions.</note> a "triple doctor" for the way he so boldly stands on the side of the **Papists**. But let it pass for now; an examination will very soon be held, and **Doctor Hoe** with his assistants <note>original: "adjuvanten"</note> may prepare themselves for it in the meantime.

But if it is not about religion, why then has it been attacked so hostilely? Why have the churches been closed and even torn down, the priests driven away, and all of this done against the granted **Letter of Majesty** <term>Majestätsbrief</term> <note>The 1609 charter granting religious freedom to Bohemian Protestants.</note>? In everything, they have shown themselves entirely contrary to the **Evangelicals**, but not in the same way against the **Papists**. What would you bet that if the noble **Count of Thurn** <note>Heinrich Matthias von Thurn, a lead figure in the Bohemian Revolt.</note> had been a **Papist**, he would not have been stripped of his office as Burgrave? The same applies to the others. Should it still not be about religion, when the **Jesuit** rule is practiced in every point, namely: **Faith is not to be kept with heretics** <note>original Latin: "Hæretico non est servanda Fides" — a Maxim Protestants frequently accused Catholics of following to justify breaking treaties.</note>? Oh, dear God, how cunning the Devil is, and yet he cannot suppress Your truth. If I were to specify here how our opponents have treated the **Evangelicals**—not only in **Prague** but also in other cities—I would have to write a considerable volume. But it is good and certain enough that experience itself speaks and proves whether this was about the region or about religion. Therefore, the lying mouths shall be put to shame, and the outcome will soon reveal who is in the right or the wrong. For things will not remain as they currently stand, but will become very different. But if you slanderers can testify against this question, then just tell me; then we shall speak further with you and stir your conscience better.

**Evangelicals** <note>The term used here for Protestants, specifically those following the Augsburg Confession.</note> were pushed out of their offices / against their privileges, and replaced with **Papist** <note>A polemical term for Roman Catholics, implying they follow the Pope rather than the Bible.</note> persons / and otherwise all sorts of tricks were practiced, just so that the Evangelicals would be bravely vexed. One had to grant the **Jesuit** and **Papist** clergy [the right] to buy many and large landed estates / so that they could bring the regions more and more [under their control]. In all this, the poor Evangelicals were content and let it pass / until finally their religion was also attacked / such that their churches were torn down / and their priests persecuted. This is how they did it / and for that reason the **Bohemians** took up their **defense** <term>Defension</term> <note>original: "Defension" — a legal and military term for a justified defensive uprising.</note>.

It is indeed well-known and revealed enough / how the poor **Bohemians** have been treated regarding religion for many years now / how hard they have been pressed / what trouble it cost / before the **Letter of Majesty** <note>original: "Majestäts Brief" — a landmark 1609 document signed by Emperor Rudolf II that granted religious freedom to the Bohemian estates.</note> was brought out / how they were trifled with. Did they not strive day and night to see how they might destroy the Evangelicals / and especially the high-ranking persons first? But now it must all be a lie / and our neighbors and supposed brothers in faith / the people of **Meissen** <note>Referring to the Saxons, who were predominantly Lutheran but often at odds with the Reformed/Calvinist influence in Bohemia.</note> / say it is nevertheless not **because of religion** <note>original: "propter religionem"</note> / just as **Doctor Hoe** <note>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), a staunch Lutheran advisor to the Elector of Saxony who opposed the Bohemian Revolt because of his hatred for Calvinism.</note> also argues and calls the Bohemians wanton enemies. But I ask **Doctor Hoe** in his conscience / whether he never perceived, back when he was still in **Prague**, that the Papists always hated the Evangelicals for the sake of religion? For why else / if they had also been Papist / would they have been so pursued? Is it then all a lie / what the Bohemians claim **regarding religion** <note>original: "de religione"</note>? Must the **Lutherans** now also help / so that the Papists remain in the right? O you young **Babylonian adulterer** <note>A harsh biblical metaphor accusing Hoe of betraying the Protestant cause by "fornicating" with the Catholic Imperialists.</note> / how you taste your harlot's forehead / how shamefully you slander your neighbor / but it will be fairly repaid to you again.

But the **Babylonian adulterer** betrays himself / in that he has revived a **Babylonian question** / in which it is supposedly proven / how one should side with the Papists much sooner and more than with those called **Calvinists**, namely in matters of religion. For the **Doctor** has written so much against the Calvinists out of envy / and he also quickly hatched ninety-nine lying **Hoe-Articles** <note>Refers to Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg's "99 Points" against the Calvinists, a famous polemical work.</note> and even more / by which he gives nothing else to understand / than that it vexes him / that the Bohemians have chosen a supposed **Calvinist King** <note>Frederick V, Elector Palatine, who was elected King of Bohemia in 1619, much to the dismay of rigid Lutherans.</note>.

# 16.

The **Evangelicals** <note>A term used by Protestants of the era to describe themselves as followers of the "Evangel" or Gospel.</note> were pushed out of their offices / contrary to their **privileges**, and these same offices were filled with **Papist** <note>A derogatory term for Roman Catholics.</note> persons / and all sorts of other tricks were practiced, so that they might bravely vex the Evangelicals. One had to allow the **Jesuits** and the Papal clergy / to purchase many large estates / so that they could bring more and more of these **regions** <note>original: *regionen*</note> under their own control. In all of this, the poor Evangelicals were content and let it pass / until finally their religion was also attacked / such that their churches were torn down / and their priests driven out. This is how they acted / and for that reason, the **Bohemians** took up their **Defense** <note>original: *Defension*; referring to the defensive alliance formed by the Bohemian estates to protect their religious rights.</note>.

It is indeed well-known and manifest enough / how the poor Bohemians have been treated regarding their religion for many years now / how they have been oppressed / and what great effort it cost / before the **Letter of Majesty** <note>The *Majestätsbrief* (1609), a document signed by Emperor Rudolf II granting religious tolerance to the Bohemian Protestants.</note> was secured. How they were trifled with! Did they not strive day and night to hunt down the Evangelicals / and especially the persons of high standing first? Now, however, it must all be a lie / and our neighbors and supposed fellow believers / the people of **Meissen** <note>Saxons; Meissen was a prominent administrative and religious center in the Electorate of Saxony.</note> / say that it is nevertheless not **because of religion** <note>original: *propter Religion*</note>, just as **Doctor Hoë** also argues, calling the Bohemians "wanton enemies." I ask **Doctor Hoë** on his conscience / whether he never perceived, while he was still in **Prague**, that the **Papists** always hated the Evangelicals for the sake of religion? For if it were for any other reason / and they had been Papists themselves / would they have persecuted them so? Is everything the Bohemians plead **regarding religion** <note>original: *de religione*</note> now a lie? Must the **Lutherans** now also help the Papists be proven right? O, you young **Babylonian adulterer** <note>A polemical insult equating the subject with the biblical Whore of Babylon, a common Reformation-era trope for the corruption of the Church.</note> / how you adorn your harlot’s forehead! How shamefully you slander your neighbor! But you shall be paid back in kind.

But the **Babylonian adulterer** betrays himself / in that he has **renewed** <note>original: *renoviret*</note> a **Babylonian question** / in which it is supposedly **proven** <note>original: *probiret*</note> / that one should side with the Papists sooner and more often than with those called **Calvinists**, namely in matters of religion. for he even betrays himself as to how matters of religion stand / and [claims] that one could much sooner choose and keep a **Papist** <note>A contemporary derogatory term for a Roman Catholic.</note> as a head [of state] than a **Calvinist** <note>A follower of the Reformed tradition of Protestantism; the author is highlighting the bitter divisions between Lutherans and Calvinists at the time.</note>. I should think so; if **Luther** were to examine his Meisseners and Saxons / he would make **Doctor Hoë** <note>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), a staunch Lutheran who famously refused to support the Bohemian Revolt because it was led by Calvinists.</note> a triple Doctor / because he stands so bravely on the **Papist** side. But let it pass for now / an examination will very soon be arranged / and Doctor Hoë with his assistants may meanwhile prepare themselves for it.

But if it is not about religion / why then has religion been so hostilely attacked / the churches closed and even torn down / the priests driven away / and all this against the granted **Letter of Majesty** <note>The *Majestätsbrief* of 1609, a document signed by Emperor Rudolf II granting religious tolerance to Bohemian Protestants.</note> / and in all things showing oneself entirely contrary to the **Evangelicals**, but not so against the **Papists**. What would you bet / if the noble **Count von Thurn** <note>Heinrich Matthias von Thurn, one of the primary leaders of the Bohemian Revolt.</note> had been a Papist / they would not have pushed him from his office as **Burgrave** / nor the others likewise. Shall it then not be called "religion" / when the **Jesuit** rule is practiced in every point, namely:

> **Faith need not be kept with heretics.** <note>original Latin: *Hæretico non est servanda Fides.* A common Protestant accusation that the Catholic Church felt it was not bound by treaties made with Protestants.</note>

O dear God, how cunning the Devil is / and yet he cannot suppress your Truth. If I were to specify here how our opposition has treated the Evangelicals / not only in **Prague** / but also in other cities / I would have to write a considerable volume / But it is good and certain enough / that experience itself speaks and proves / whether it was a matter of **Region** or **Religion** <note>A play on the legal principle *Cuius regio, eius religio*—"Whose realm, his religion"—which governed the official faith of a territory.</note> / Therefore, the lying mouths shall be put to shame / and one will also experience in the end / who was right or wrong / For it will not remain as it stands now / but will become quite different. If you **Calumniators** <note>Slanderers; those who spread false accusations.</note> can testify against this question / then tell me again / and then we shall speak further with you / and touch your conscience more deeply.

## IV.
## Whether the Bohemians originally recruited their soldiery against the Emperor / whether they are so bloodthirsty / and with what conscience they took up their **Defense**.

<meta>This page continues the polemic against those who claim the Bohemian conflict is purely political. It focuses on the specific grievances of the Bohemian Protestants, such as the seizure of offices and the destruction of churches, and levels a harsh personal attack against the Saxon court preacher Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg for his perceived betrayal of the Protestant cause.</meta>

The **Evangelicals** <note>A term used by Protestants of the Reformation era—both Lutherans and Reformed—to describe themselves.</note> were ousted from their offices—contrary to their privileges—and replaced by **Papist** <note>A contemporary derogatory term for Roman Catholics.</note> persons. Various other little tricks were practiced just to bravely vex the **Evangelicals**. One had to permit the **Jesuit-furies** <note>A pejorative pun in the original German (*Jesuwüten*) combining "Jesuit" with the word for "raging" or "fury."</note> and the Popish clergy to purchase many large estates, so that they might bring more and more regions under their control. In all this, the poor **Evangelicals** remained peaceful and let it pass, until finally their religion itself was attacked, their churches were torn down, and their priests were driven away. This is how they acted, and for this reason, the **Bohemians** <note>The Protestant estates of the Kingdom of Bohemia who revolted against Habsburg rule in 1618, sparking the Thirty Years' War.</note> turned to their own defense.

It is indeed known throughout the land and obvious enough how the poor **Bohemians** have been treated regarding their religion for many years now; how hard they have been pressed, and what effort it took before the **Letter of Majesty** <note>The *Majestätsbrief* of 1609, a document signed by Emperor Rudolf II that granted religious freedom to the Bohemian estates.</note> was secured. How they were trifled with! Did they not strive day and night to figure out how to ruin the **Evangelicals**, especially the persons of high standing first? But now, it must all be a lie; our neighbors and supposed brothers in faith, the **Meisseners** <note>People of the Margraviate of Meissen in Saxony, referring here to the Saxon Lutherans who refused to support the Bohemian cause.</note>, say it is nevertheless not **for the sake of religion** <note>original Latin: "propter Religionem"</note>—just as **Doctor Hoë** <note>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), the influential and staunchly anti-Calvinist court preacher in Dresden.</note> also argues, calling the **Bohemians** "willful enemies."

But I ask **Doctor Hoë** on his conscience: did he never perceive, while he was still in Prague, that the **Papists** always hated the **Evangelicals** for the sake of religion? For why else—had they also been **Papist**—would they have been so persecuted? Is everything the **Bohemians** claim regarding religion now a lie? Must the **Lutherans** now help the **Papists** be in the right? O, you young Babylonian adulterer! How you adorn your harlot's brow; how shamefully you slander your neighbor! But you shall be paid back in full.

But the Babylonian adulterer betrays himself, in that he has revived a "Babylonian question" wherein he attempts to prove, in a supposedly wise manner, how one should hold with the **Papists** sooner and more so than with the so-called **Calvinists** <note>Followers of the Reformed tradition; the author is criticizing the "Lutheran-Catholic" alliance against the "Calvinist" rebels.</note>, namely in matters of religion. This is why the Doctor wrote so much against the **Calvinists** out of envy, and why he quickly hatched ninety-nine lying **Breeches Articles** <note>A reference to Hoe’s famous polemic *99 Errors of the Calvinists*; the author uses the mocking term "Hosen Artickell" (Breeches Articles) to disparage the work.</note> and others, through which he gives the others nothing to understand except that it galls him that the **Bohemians** have elected a supposedly **Calvinist** King <note>Frederick V of the Palatinate, known as the "Winter King," whose election by the Bohemians was opposed by many Lutherans.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the argument that the conflict in Bohemia is fundamentally religious. In this way, he also betrays his own intentions regarding religion, claiming that a **Papist** <note>A contemporary, often derogatory term for a Roman Catholic.</note> could much sooner be chosen and retained as a leader than a **Calvinist**. If it is not about religion, then why is there so much dispute over religious matters? **In the year [16]17**, they celebrated the **Jubilee** <note>The 100th anniversary of the Reformation in 1617.</note> against the Pope; yet afterward, they formed alliances with his **adulterers** <note>In the polemical language of the time, "adultery" often referred to "spiritual adultery"—betraying the true faith to associate with the enemy.</note> and finally allowed it to be publicly declared that one should hold with them rather than with others. Is that what it means to be purely **Evangelical** and **Lutheran**? I believe that if **Luther** were to examine his people of Meissen and **Saxony**, he would make **Doctor Hoë** <note>Matthias Hoe von Hoenegg (1580–1645), the powerful court preacher to the Elector of Saxony, who famously distrusted Calvinists more than Catholics.</note> a "triple doctor" for the way he so bravely stands on the side of the **Papists**. But let it pass for now; an examination will very soon be conducted, and **Doctor Hoë** with his assistants may meanwhile prepare themselves for it.

But if it is not about religion, why then has religion been so hostilely attacked? Why were the churches closed and even torn down, and the priests driven away? All of this was done in violation of the granted **Letter of Majesty** <note>The *Majestätsbrief* of 1609, a document signed by Emperor Rudolf II that guaranteed religious freedom to the Bohemian estates.</note> and in a manner that showed complete opposition to the **Evangelicals**, but not toward the **Papists**. What would it matter? If the noble **Count of Thurn** <note>Heinrich Matthias von Thurn, one of the main leaders of the Bohemian Revolt.</note> had been a **Papist**, he would not have been stripped of his office as **Burgrave**; and the same applies to the others. Should it still not be considered a matter of religion when the **Jesuit-fury** <note>original: *Jesuwütische*—a pun on "Jesuit" and "raging fury."</note> rule is practiced in every detail? Namely: **Faith is not to be kept with heretics.** <note>original: "Haeretico non est servanda Fides." This was a common Protestant accusation that Catholics felt legally justified in breaking promises made to non-Catholics.</note> Oh, dear God, how cunning the Devil is! And yet, he cannot suppress your truth. If I were to specify here how our opponents have treated the **Evangelicals**, not only in **Prague** but also in other cities, I would have to write a quite substantial volume. But it is good and certain enough that experience itself speaks and proves whether it was a matter of the region or of religion. Therefore, the lying mouths shall be put to shame, and the outcome will soon reveal who was right or wrong. For things will not remain as they are now, but will become very different indeed. If you slanderers can testify against this question, then just tell me again; then we shall speak further with you and touch your conscience more deeply.
