And **Revelation 14, verse 9**: If anyone worships the beast and its image / and receives the mark on his forehead / or on his hand / he shall drink of the wine of the wrath of God / which is poured out and pure in the cup of his wrath / and will be tormented with fire and sulfur / before the holy angels / and before the Lamb / and the smoke of their torment will rise from eternity to eternity / and they have no rest day and night / who have worshiped the beast and its image / and if anyone has received the mark of its name.

Come here now, Mr. Doctor, with all your <term>slanderers</term> <note>original: "Calumnianten"</note> and other rabble / who are and stand so bravely and boldly on the Emperor’s side / and read there your <term>nativity</term> <note>original: "Nativitäten"; in this context, a prophetic judgment of one's destiny or "horoscope" of their fate.</note> / which the angel—not the supposed **Felgenhawer** <note>original: "Felgenhawer"; likely a reference to an adversary or a pseudonym of the time.</note>—sets before you / look upon it / and note very well / what your wage will be.

But what does it actually mean to "take the mark of the beast"? It means nothing other / than that one calls oneself after the beast and its name / is empowered and <term>privileged</term> by it / allows oneself to be appointed against God and against the saints / screams and bares teeth constantly: "Emperor! Emperor! Emperor!" / meanwhile God may be with his saints wherever He wants / as long as one gives to the Emperor what is not his / and in contrast takes from God what is His / as long as one has much to boast about from the Emperor / how one is an **Imperial Commissioner** over the Bohemians / how one is made a <term>Count Palatine</term> <note>original: "Comes Palatinus"</note> by the Emperor / because of the faithful services one has rendered him / and whatever more of this filth there is. Does this not mean worshiping the beast and its image / and confessing oneself guilty of serving the beast / through which God and his saints are blasphemed and shamed? See, you servants of the dragon and worshipers of the <term>door</term> <note>original: "Thuranbeter"; likely a derogatory term for courtiers who wait at the "door" of power, or a play on "throne-worshiper."</note> / how you serve the devil / and blaspheme God in heaven along with the beast. Now read also what your wage shall be / and enjoy it / with the beast and the dragon / since you would not let yourselves be warned.

Yes, if you were so poor, I say, then you could ask the Emperor for a pair of old trousers as a gift and for a piece of bread to live on.

Now, you people of understanding, who does not recognize the Devil of Greed, who is not satisfied with one master but wishes to serve two masters, so that he might have better pay? For he writes himself down as the Emperor's servant. That is right, my Lord Doctor, take an office from the Emperor as well, for it is currently a little office that is worth the least.

You have a stinking heart, and you cannot think highly enough of yourself; you are so unrestrained that you cannot even tolerate a poor little hen <note>The author uses the diminutive "Hänichen" (little hen/chicken) to represent a humble, pious neighbor or fellow believer whom Hoë oppresses.</note> nesting beside you. Currently they—those blind asses—sing the *Te Deum laudamus* <note>original: *Te Deum laudamus*; a traditional hymn meaning "We praise Thee, O God."</note>, and they do so because they have overcome the saints of the Most High. They believe nothing else than what Christ said: they think they are doing God a service when they kill the saints of God. Oh, dear LORD Jesus, how truly You prophesied! For these people want to know everything and yet they know neither You nor Your Father. Therefore, You shall surely find them in Your fierce wrath. Now our enemies sing the *Te Deum laudamus* <note>original: *Te Deum laudamus*</note> with their mouths, but in their hearts, they mean the Emperor, whom they praise, honor, and worship. Yet he is not of their *religion* <note>original: *religion*</note>, nor does he care for them from his heart—for how could he truly care for his opposition? That would surely be against the most holy Pope. But I know for certain in my GOD: within two years at the longest, one shall hear our enemies singing and howling the *ejulamus* <note>original: *ejulamus*; a wordplay contrasting the "laudamus" (we praise) of the hymn with the Latin *ejulare*, meaning "we wail" or "we lament."</note>. Then you shall experience whether the LORD has been with me, or if I have merely dreamed it.

...defile [themselves] with the Babylonian Whore <note>A common Protestant Reformation-era term for the Roman Catholic Church, derived from the Book of Revelation.</note>, neither in the smallest nor the least [degree], for I have testified clearly enough to them how much depends upon it—namely, that God or the Lamb <note>Jesus Christ.</note> has removed the names of those who agree with the Beast from the Book of Life. As dearly as one holds the salvation of their soul, so dearly let them hold the warning of Jesus Christ, his most faithful Savior, who bought him and us all at a very high price. And let him not mix himself into these affairs at the risk of his soul, even if he should lose his life over it; for it is better to die uprightly and innocently with Christ than to live shamefully in a hypocritical manner.

You honest and upright people of Meissen and Saxony, you who do not worship the Beast or his image—I do not mean you with a single breath [when I speak] of this Bohemian Cupbearer and Palatine Seneschal <note>These refer to the ceremonial court titles of Frederick V of the Palatinate, the "Winter King" of Bohemia. The author is likely distinguishing the common people from the high-level political figures and their detractors.</note>, but I mean the ungodly mockers. Do not follow the young Pope in Dresden <note>A scathing nickname for Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg, the Elector’s powerful court preacher.</note>, who commands you to enter into and make an alliance with the Popes, but rather remember what Jesus Christ says in his Revelation, where he speaks through his angel: "Go out from her, my people, so that you do not become partakers of her sins," etc. <note>A reference to Revelation 18:4.</note> Now judge as sensible people: is it better to obey God or a man? If I speak with the Scripture, then believe me; but if I speak of myself, then you need not believe me, for I would be a liar like Doctor Hoë is. 

I also wish to have warned you in an outward manner: do not hang your heart upon that wealth; it will be of no use to you on the Day of the Lord’s wrath, but rather a cause of your death. For I know how it went in Bohemia <note>Referring to the devastation following the Battle of White Mountain in 1620, which began the Thirty Years' War.</note>; believe me, poor soul that I am, I know it, and you may believe me more than when Doctor Hoë speaks. Your Graces, only believe! It grieves my heart to think of the misfortune that will come upon you, for there will be no sparing then. Oh, what a terrifying word is that which Jerusalem cries out unceasingly to God, saying: "Repay our neighbors SEVENFOLD <note>original: *SIEBENFELTIG*</note>, more than us" <note>A reference to Psalm 79:12, a plea for divine vengeance against enemies who have destroyed the holy city.</note>. 

Who is there now who could describe the coming misfortune? Or what a terrifying anguish of heart will strike you? For I know how it was when I was in the midst of the enemies at every moment. Now the Lord knows His own; He will also rescue them and bring them to His glory, so that they will rule with Him eternally in His coming Kingdom over all devils and the ungodly. This I wish for all the chosen and the good-hearted. 

AMEN.

# CONCLUSION.

### Whether it is right that I have spoken so bluntly:

I know full well that many a sensible person—to say nothing of the mockers—will say that I have gone far too far in these matters and have not even spared those of the highest rank, which, in a worldly sense <note>original: "politicè"</note>, ought not to be done. But to those I give an answer from the Scriptures, namely that which the Prophet says: that the names of the godless shall be a curse to the chosen; and furthermore, let one read Isaiah 65:15. In the Book of Revelation <note>original: "Apocalypsi"</note> is that beautiful tribute and praise of the Beast; after reading it, let someone tell me again whether I have committed a **crime of treason** <term>original: "Crimen læfæ Majestatis"; a legal term for insulting or betraying the sovereign</term>. Truly, I have done so just as little as Christ did when he called Herod a fox, and called the Pharisees by many names, even calling them all children of the Devil together. Should Herod not be called a bloodhound because he beheaded John? Was one required to look upon his Royal Majesty and spare him then? Oh no; whoever is of God testifies to the truth and fears neither Emperor nor Prince, even if it were an Elector <note>The high-ranking German princes who elected the Holy Roman Emperor; here specifically referring to the Elector of Saxony.</note>; for with GOD nothing—no external person—counts for anything. For if they do not spare God and his saints, how should the Truth then spare them? For it is said: "One turn deserves another." 

In addition, let the sensible person read the fourth book of Esdras, chapter 11 <note>An apocryphal book of the Bible, very popular in the 17th century for its apocalyptic prophecies</note>, concerning the Lion, how he speaks against the Eagle and rebukes him for the sake of his unrighteousness. Therefore, I speak and rebuke within my **calling** <note>original: "vocation"</note> according to the Scriptures and the holy prophecies of the same. If there is anyone who dares to stand against the Lion, let him do it at his own peril, for he is not a man but the **Wind of the Most High** <term>original: "Ventus Altissimi"; a reference to the divine spirit or breath of God</term>, the Spirit of the Highest, as the Angel says. And above all this, why should I not be in the right, rather than the mockers who are of the Devil? For GOD has sent me through His Spirit and anointed me that I should testify to the truth. Have the **slanderers** <note>original: "Calumnianten"</note> not acted wickedly enough against our God-fearing King FREDERICK <note>Frederick V of the Palatinate (1596–1632), the "Winter King" of Bohemia and a hero to the radical Protestant cause</note>? What kind of ghastly, terrifying insults and blasphemies have they poured out? His dear friend, the high-born Prince from Transylvania <note>original: "Siebenburgen"; referring to Gabriel Bethlen, a Prince of Transylvania and ally to the Protestant cause</note>, has been scolded as a beggar; how they have shamed the highly-praised Union <note>The Protestant Union, a coalition of Protestant German states</note>, which was nonetheless **constituted** by GOTT Himself! How they have slandered other Princes and Counts, whom they have even called "sons of whores" altogether. GOD is my witness, I am heartily ashamed that I should have to recount publicly how they [treated] our...

...about **FREDERICK** <note>Refers to Frederick V of the Palatinate, the "Winter King," a key Protestant figure.</note> was intended. For you have acted as people forgotten by honor—as loose, frivolous, and shameful thieves of reputation and murderers of names, and indeed, all of you are master-liars of the Devil. I, however, do this based on the foundation of the Scriptures. Therefore, I follow the teaching of the wise man who says: *Do not answer a fool according to his folly, lest you also become like him.* Proverbs 26:4. Now, I have done just that, for I have not brought childish nonsense or "monkey-play" <note>original: "Affenspiel"; refers to buffoonery or deceptive behavior</note> to the stage, but rather Scripture, Scripture! Take note, you slanderers: if I had brought forth external fantasies and nonsense as you have, I would have become like you. But no! Yet the wise man also says, on the other hand: *Answer a fool according to his folly, lest he think himself too wise.* <note>Proverbs 26:5</note> Now, I have done this as well, which seems to please you well enough. Look to it, gentlemen; the power of **GOD** will certainly be a match for you, and you will surely not be able to resist Him. 

You "honorable" fire-foxes <note>original: "BrandFüchse"; a derogatory term for agitators or those who spread "fire" (heresy/sedition)</note> call the Protestant Union a "rabbit alliance" and a "patched-together beggar’s coat," and call Gabriel <note>Likely referring to Gabriel Bethlen, Prince of Transylvania, a Protestant ally.</note> a beggar, and the noble Prince of Orange <note>Maurice of Nassau, Prince of Orange, a leader of the Dutch Revolt against Spain.</note> an "Orange fire-fox," and other such things. Now, you have printed and sold enough pasquils <term>original: "Pasquillen"; anonymous lampoons or satirical political pamphlets</term>, especially in Leipzig, where that beautiful "praise-speech" was printed. One cannot hold it against the Papists so much as against these "fine Lutherans." 

Well then, go ahead and make a better alliance with the Papists; you will certainly receive the "best" reward for it, because you are committing adultery with Jezebel. This is exactly as it is written in Revelation 2:22-23: that **GOD** will cast her adulterers into a bed of misery and strike her children dead. Among these, the Emperor and the Spaniard are surely the most prominent, for they are indeed the Pope's sons and children; no living person can deny this. (Whether the Pope is not himself Jezebel, I leave to **Doctor Hoë’s** conscience to say, as well as his own sermons and writings). And after them come the Laodiceans <note>The "lukewarm" church mentioned in Revelation 3:14-22; here used to insult Lutherans who did not fully commit to the Protestant cause.</note>. Let **Doctor Matz of Dresden** <note>A mocking nickname for Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg; "Matz" was a common diminutive for Matthias but also a slang term for a fool or simpleton.</note> deny that Jezebel is the Pope and the aforementioned are his adulterers, and I will rely upon...

## To the Silesians..

Dear Neighbors, it surprises me little that you let yourselves be turned away from the Head <note>A reference to Christ as the head of the Church</note> through the counsel of the Devil’s companions of Belial <note>A biblical term for the personification of worthlessness or the Devil</note>, who, by means of the wretched, thinks to escape thus unforgotten. However, because you are also somewhat blind, and neither cold nor warm <note>A reference to Revelation 3:15-16, rebuking those who are spiritually lukewarm</note>, and are unwilling for Christ’s sake to suffer with the poor Bohemians, Moravians, Austrians, Hungarians, and Palatines <note>These groups represent the Protestant estates and territories involved in the early stages of the Thirty Years' War</note>; well now, let it herewith be foretold and announced to you: that unless you turn back in time with those who suffer, before the **LORD** completely departs from your borders, you should know for certain that I shall be no lying prophet to you; you shall be flogged <note>original: "gesteupet"; referring to a public corporal punishment or scourging</note> three times more than the others. Since it is not possible that the children remain spared when it goes over the mothers. Which, by virtue of my office, I herewith testify to you, according to which you must conduct yourselves. Blessed is he who lets himself be warned and hears what Truth says. Fare you well.

->The Lion from the Forest..<note>original: "Leo ex Sylva"; a pseudonym often used by the radical theologian and writer Friedrich Breckling, or similar prophetic figures of the era</note>

<summary>The author issues a stern prophetic warning to the Silesians, accusing them of spiritual lukewarmness and predicting they will suffer a triple punishment for failing to stand with their Protestant neighbors.</summary>

# Copy of a Letter sent by Dr. Hoë <note>Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg (1580–1645), a powerful and controversial Lutheran court preacher in Saxony known for his staunch opposition to Calvinism and his surprising diplomatic ties to the Catholic Emperor</note> to Emperor FERDINAND <note>Ferdinand II (1578–1637), the Holy Roman Emperor whose policies helped spark the Thirty Years' War</note> /

->In the past year, 1620.<-

<image-desc>A small decorative scrollwork ornament with stylized foliage and a central mask-like face.</image-desc>

<image-desc>Decorative initial A depicting a figure seated within an architectural or floral frame.</image-desc>Most Illustrious, Most Mighty, Most Unconquerable Roman Emperor, also King of Hungary and Bohemia, to Your Roman Imperial Majesty are offered—alongside devout prayer—my most humble and dutiful services at all times. Most Gracious Emperor, King, and Lord: that Your Imperial Majesty has accepted my most humble congratulation with such Imperial grace, and has shown yourself so most graciously of your own accord toward me; for this I thank Your Imperial Majesty in the most humble manner, wishing from the Most High <note>A common reference to God</note> that He may richly reward Your Imperial Majesty for such high Imperial grace with a long life, a happy reign, a heartfelt victory over your enemies, and all welfare of soul and body. Regarding Your Imperial Majesty’s most gracious desire that I should continue in my most humble devotion <note>original: "Devotion"; here meaning loyalty or service to the crown</note>, [I shall] before si- <note>The text cuts off mid-word, continuing on the next page; the catchword suggests "sicherlich" or "sichere," meaning "surely" or "secure."</note>

<summary>This page contains the formal opening of a letter from the Lutheran theologian Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg to Emperor Ferdinand II, expressing deep gratitude for the Emperor's favor and offering prayers for his victory and health during the early stages of the Thirty Years' War.</summary>
<keywords>Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg, Ferdinand II, 1620, Imperial diplomacy, Thirty Years' War, Lutheran-Catholic relations, Holy Roman Empire</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues the correspondence from Dr. Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg to Emperor Ferdinand II. The previous page (page 81) began the formal address and expressions of gratitude. This page (page 86) continues the letter, discussing the diplomatic mission of his brother, Maximilian, and affirming the author's unwavering loyalty to the Emperor based on biblical principles. The catchword "Welches" at the bottom of the previous page would have led into the first word of this page, though the OCR starts mid-word.</meta>

<page-num>86.</page-num>

...[most gra]cious Your Imperial Majesty, I most obediently perform and provide that which I am able to do according to my best knowledge and conscience. Since it serves Your Imperial Majesty’s most gracious pleasure and benefit in the current state of affairs, I shall never and at no time cease from doing so. Should my brother **Maximilian Hoe von Hoenegg** <note>Maximilian was the brother of the court preacher Matthias; unlike his brother the theologian, Maximilian served in more secular and administrative roles</note> arrive here, I will follow Your Imperial Majesty's command with the utmost humility, and hopefully achieve so much through moving persuasion that Your Imperial Majesty will be most graciously satisfied with him. I do not doubt that he was forced into this commission against his will; it is easy to count on one's fingers why my brother from the knightly estate was deputized for this, namely that he should travel to the Electorate of Saxony <note>Chur Sachsen: The territories ruled by the Elector of Saxony, a key Protestant power during the Thirty Years' War</note>. I fear, however, that in this case as well, many thoughts of the subtle enemies will come to nothing <note>Literally "turn to water"</note>.

For this is certainly true: that I remain by the command of the only Savior of the world:
> Render unto Caesar what is Caesar's, and unto God what is God's <note>A famous citation of Matthew 22:21, often used in this era to justify absolute obedience to secular rulers</note>;

from this I will not be moved, whether by grace or disgrace, friend or foe, silver or gold, men or angels—indeed, by nothing at all, neither high nor low. And I believe firmly that He who has properly set the Imperial and Royal crowns upon Your Imperial Majesty's head will also powerfully and mightily protect and uphold Your Imperial Majesty, strike all of Your Imperial Majesty's willful enemies on the cheeks, smash their teeth, turn them back, and suddenly bring them to shame. Amen. May the **GOD** of all gods and King of all kings grant this. Amen.

<summary>In this conclusion to his letter, Dr. Hoë affirms his absolute loyalty to the Catholic Emperor Ferdinand II, citing the biblical mandate to "render unto Caesar." He also mentions the diplomatic mission of his brother, Maximilian, to the Electorate of Saxony, framing his political support as a religious duty.</summary>

<keywords>Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg, Emperor Ferdinand II, Maximilian Hoe von Hoenegg, Electorate of Saxony, biblical obedience, Render unto Caesar, Thirty Years' War diplomacy</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
<meta>This page concludes the letter from Dr. Matthias Hoë von Hoenegg to the Emperor, which began on the previous pages. The text shifts from the formal conclusion of the correspondence to a defensive introduction for a following section of his work, addressing his critics and "slanderers" during the early stages of the Thirty Years' War.</meta>

<page-num>87.</page-num>

All of which I should not withhold from Your Imperial Majesty in the most submissive manner, most obediently praying that Your Imperial Majesty deign to remain my and my family’s most gracious Emperor, King, and Lord henceforth. Given the 1st of March, Old Style Calendar <note>The Julian calendar, which was still used by Protestants in Germany at this time, while Catholics had adopted the Gregorian "New Style" calendar</note>, in the YEAR 1620.

->Your Roman Imperial and Royal Majesty’s<-
->Most Submissive<-
->and Duty-bound Servant,<-
->**Matthias Hoë Von**<-
->**Hoeneggk, Doctor**<-

Upon this letter, the Lord Doctor wishes this second part to be taken as an answer, along with all other slanderers regarding me as a "Bohemian Cup-bearer" and "Palatine Seneschal" <note>These were likely mocking titles or accusations leveled against Hoë von Hoenegg by his political and theological opponents, suggesting he was a servant to foreign interests in Bohemia or the Palatinate rather than a loyal theologian</note>, for which reason I wish to have politely asked them with these words:

> Eat what I offer; go and drink what I pour for you. <note>original Latin: "CoMeDIte qVæ offero, Ite & bIbIte qVæ propIno VobIs." This sentence is a chronogram; the capitalized Roman numerals (C, M, D, I, V, I, I, I, V, I, V, I) add up to the year 1621, a common wordplay in 17th-century literature to encode a date within a phrase.</note>

<summary>Dr. Matthias Hoë von Hoenegg concludes his formal letter of loyalty to the Emperor and transitions into a defensive response against his political and religious critics using a Latin chronogram.</summary>
<keywords>Matthias Hoë von Hoenegg, Emperor Ferdinand II, Old Style Calendar, 1620, Thirty Years' War, Chronogram, Polemics, Bohemia, Palatinate</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>This page provides a table of contents or "Index of Questions" for the subsequent section of the work. It transitions from the personal defense of Dr. Matthias Hoë von Hoenegg into a broader theological and political analysis of the Bohemian Revolt, a pivotal conflict that triggered the Thirty Years' War.</meta>

<page-num>88.</page-num>

## The index of all questions to be printed hereafter.

1. Why God has punished the Bohemians so, and what the cause might be?
2. Whether the Bohemians also committed **treason** <note>original Latin: "Crimen læsæ Majestatis," referring to the crime of violating the majesty of the sovereign</note>, and whether they had the power to make use of their liberties.
3. Whether the Bohemians were concerned with territory or rather with religion.
4. Whether the Bohemians initially deployed their army against the Emperor; whether they are so bloodthirsty, and with what sort of conscience they took up their **defense**.
5. Whether the Bohemians, with a good conscience, **politically** <note>original: "Politice"</note> rejected **FERDINAND** <note>Ferdinand II, the Catholic Holy Roman Emperor</note> and elected **FREDERICK, the Elector** <note>Frederick V of the Palatinate, the Protestant "Winter King"</note> as King.
6. Whether Elector Frederick, Count Palatine of the Rhine, is still to be considered King in Bohemia by those who are sensible?
7. Whether the **Union** <note>The Protestant Union, a coalition of Protestant German states</note> is from God, where its basis lies in Scripture, and what is to be thought of it altogether?
8. Whether one should respect the authorities according to a political, external conscience, and yet help to persecute fellow believers against one's inner conscience.
9. Whether the Bohemians have then become **Calvinists**?
10. Whether the Bohemians alone have sinned, and whether those who sided with the Papists <note>Catholics</note> will now receive this as their reward?
11. Whether and how the Bohemians and the Kingdom can be compared to **Jerusalem**, and also be compared in a similar manner according to [divine] wisdom?
12. How it then fared for **Jerusalem** and how it will fare, and how it will later fare for other nations?
13. How things will be regarding religion in the future.
14. How it stands with the Roman Eagle <note>The symbol of the Holy Roman Empire</note>, which is now with the House of Austria, and otherwise with the House of Austria, according to the secret wisdom of God?
15. Who then has triumphed in the Bohemian matter—the Emperor or the Bohemians—according to a secret understanding?
16. Whether, speaking **theologically** and **plainly**, the Bohemians are in the wrong because they resisted the authorities, and whether a Christian should also wage war?
17. Whether Doctor **Hoë** also did wrong in that he became a **Count Palatine** <note>original: "Comes Palatinus," specifically an Imperial Count Palatine, a title of honor and legal authority granted by the Emperor</note>?

->E N D.<-

<summary>This page lists seventeen questions that the author intends to address, covering the legality, morality, and theological implications of the Bohemian Revolt and the election of Frederick V.</summary>
<keywords>Bohemia, treason, Ferdinand II, Frederick V, Protestant Union, Calvinism, Jerusalem, Roman Eagle, House of Austria, Matthias Hoë von Hoenegg, Count Palatine</keywords>

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>This page serves as the formal title page for the third part of the work previously outlined in the index. It marks a shift from legalistic defense to apocalyptic interpretation, using the biblical imagery of a lion and an eagle to frame the ongoing conflict of the Thirty Years' War.</meta>

# THE ROARING LION
<note>original Latin: "Leo Rugiens"</note>
->in<-
->The Third Part of the Prophetic Decision on the Bohemian War<-
<note>original Latin: "Decisionis Propheticæ Belli Bohemici PARTE TERTIA"</note>

# The Third Part of the
->Judgment on the Bohemian Affair,<-
->Completed and finished from the prophecies of the<-
->prophets of the Old and New Testaments and by the grace of GOD<-
->from the beginning until the end, clearly testified in all respects,<-
->By<-
->The Lion from the Forest, roaring at the Eagle,<-
<note>original Latin: "Leonem ex Sylva, rugientem ad Aquilam." This is a pseudonym; the "Lion" likely represents the Protestant cause or the Elector Palatine, while the "Eagle" represents the Habsburg Holy Roman Empire.</note>

->*The Lion, strengthened in the Spirit of the Lord, shall triumph; however, the Eagle shall be burned by the heat of the fire of the Lord of Hosts and shall perish.*<-
<note>original Latin: "Leo in Spiritu Domini corroboratus, triumphabit, Aquila autem fervore ignis Domini Zebaoth cremabitur & peribit."</note>

->*Daniel 7:21; Revelation 12:7.*<-

> The little horn and the beast will fight with the saints and overcome them and hold the victory, until the judgment is held.

->*Psalm 79:2–3; Revelation 11:7–9.*<-

> They have given the corpses of your servants to the birds under the heavens to devour, and the flesh of your saints to the beasts in the land. They have shed blood around Jerusalem like water, and there was no one to bury them.

## The Third Part of the Prophetic Flower.
<note>original Latin: "Floris Prophetici Dritter Theyl."</note>

->*2 Esdras 12*<-
->The VVinD of the Most HIgh In the LIon shaLL sCatter the VVICkeD.<-
<note>original Latin: "VentVs ALtIsfIMI In Leone DIsperget InIqVos." This sentence is a chronogram where the capitalized Latin numerals (V, V, L, I, I, M, I, I, L, D, I, I, V) add up to the year 1621.</note>

<summary>This is the title page for the third part of a polemical work that interprets the Bohemian Revolt and the Thirty Years' War through the lens of biblical prophecy. It uses the symbolic "Roaring Lion" to predict the eventual triumph of the Protestant cause over the Imperial "Eagle."</summary>

<keywords>Bohemian War, Roaring Lion, Imperial Eagle, prophecy, 1621, chronogram, Book of Daniel, Book of Revelation, 2 Esdras</keywords>

--- Page 123 ---
<meta>This page provides a table of contents or a list of "disputed questions" for the third part of the work. It transitions from the prophetic imagery of the previous page into a specific set of political, theological, and legal inquiries regarding the Bohemian Revolt (1618–1620) and the early stages of the Thirty Years' War.</meta>

<page-num>88.</page-num>

# The index of all questions shall be printed hereafter.

1. Why God has so punished the Bohemians / what the cause might be?
2. Whether the Bohemians also committed a crime of high treason <note>original Latin: *crimen læsæ Majestatis*</note>, and whether they had the power to make use of their liberties<unclear>n</unclear>?
3. Whether it was a matter of territory <note>original: *Region*</note> or religion for the Bohemians.
4. Whether the Bohemians initially recruited their army against the Emperor; whether they are so bloodthirsty; [and] with what sort of conscience they took up their defense.
5. Whether the Bohemians, with a good political conscience, rejected **FERDINAND** <note>Ferdinand II, the Holy Roman Emperor.</note> and elected **FREDERICK** the Elector <note>Frederick V of the Palatinate, known as the "Winter King."</note> as King.
6. Whether Elector Frederick, Count Palatine of the Rhine, is still King of Bohemia and should be considered as such by those of understanding?
7. Whether the Union <note>The Protestant Union, a coalition of Protestant German states formed in 1608.</note> is from God / where its foundation lies in Scripture, and what is to be thought of it in all respects?
8. Whether, according to an external political conscience, one should respect the authorities and help to drive away fellow believers against one's internal conscience.
9. Whether the Bohemians have become Calvinist?
10. Whether the Bohemians alone have sinned / and whether those who sided with the Papists <note>A contemporary term for Roman Catholics, often used polemically by Protestants.</note> will now receive [misery] as their thanks?
11. Whether and how the Bohemians and the Kingdom [of Bohemia] can be compared with Jerusalem / and is also compared in like manner through [divine] wisdom?
12. How it then fared for Jerusalem and will fare [in the future] / and how it will go thereafter for other nations?
13. What the state of religion will be in the future.
14. How it stands with the Roman Eagle <note>The symbol of the Holy Roman Empire and the Habsburg dynasty.</note> that is now with the House of Austria, and otherwise with the House of Austria according to the secret wisdom of God?
15. Who then has triumphed in the Bohemian affair / the Emperor or the Bohemians, according to a secret understanding?
16. Whether—speaking theologically and simply <note>original Latin: *Theologice vnd simpliciter*</note>—the Bohemians are in the wrong because they defended themselves against the authorities / and whether a Christian should also wage war?
17. Whether Doctor Hoe <note>Matthias Hoë von Hoënegg (1580–1645), a prominent and controversial Lutheran court preacher in Saxony who supported the Emperor against the Bohemians.</note> has also done wrong by becoming a Count Palatine <note>original Latin: *Comes Palatinus*</note>?

->T H E  E N D.<-

<summary>This page lists seventeen central questions that the author intends to answer, covering the legal, political, and prophetic justifications for the Bohemian Revolt and the election of Frederick V. It frames the conflict not just as a local war, but as a theological event comparable to the fate of biblical Jerusalem.</summary>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>The previous page presented a table of contents or "disputed questions" regarding the legality and theological implications of the Bohemian Revolt. This page is the formal title page for the third part of that work, employing heavy apocalyptic and symbolic language to frame the conflict between the Protestant forces and the Holy Roman Empire.</meta>

# THE ROARING LION
->in the<-
## Prophetic Decision on the Bohemian War
### PART THREE.

# Third Part of the
## Decision regarding the Bohemian Affair,

->Completed and finished from the prophecies of the<-
->prophets of the Old and New Testaments and, by the grace of GOD,<-
->testified quite clearly from beginning to end;<-
->By<-

### The Lion from the Forest, roaring at the Eagle,
<note>In this context, the "Lion" often symbolizes the Protestant cause or Frederick V, while the "Eagle" represents the Holy Roman Empire and the House of Habsburg.</note>

->*The Lion, strengthened in the Spirit of the Lord, shall triumph; however, the Eagle*<-
->*shall be consumed by the heat of the fire of the Lord of Hosts and shall perish.*<-
<note>original Latin: *Leo in Spiritu Domini corroboratus, triumphabit, Aquila autem fervore ignis Domini Zebaoth cremabitur & peribit.*</note>
->Daniel 7:21, Revelation 12:7.<-

> The little horn and the beast will fight against the saints and overcome them and hold the victory, until the judgment is held.

->Psalm 79:2, Revelation 11:7, 8, 9.<-

> They have given the corpses of your servants to the birds under the heavens for food, and the flesh of your saints to the beasts in the land. They have shed blood around Jerusalem like water, and there was no one who buried them.

### Third Part of the Prophetic Flower.

->4 Esdras 12.<-
<note>Also known as 2 Esdras in many English Bibles; chapter 12 contains a famous vision of a Lion rebuking an Eagle.</note>

->The WInD of the Most HIgh In the LIon shaLL sCatter the WICkeD.<-
<note>original Latin: *VentVs ALtIssIMI In Leone DIsperget InIqVos.* This is a chronogram where the capitalized letters (V, V, L, I, I, M, I, I, L, D, I, I, V) represent Roman numerals that, when added together, signify the year 1621.</note>

<sig>i</sig>

<summary>This title page introduces the third installment of a prophetic interpretation of the Bohemian Revolt (1618–1620), framing the conflict as a biblical struggle between a "Lion" and an "Eagle." It uses apocalyptic scripture and a 1621 chronogram to justify the Protestant cause against the Habsburg Empire.</summary>
<keywords>Bohemian Revolt, Thirty Years' War, prophecy, apocalyptic literature, Lion of Judah, Holy Roman Empire, Frederick V, chronogram, 1621</keywords>
